## Authority and bounded scope

[Architecture](../docs/windows-time-architecture.md) REQ-10, DEC-8 and §7.2 require source selection with majority agreement and an independently configured minimum; CHECK-7/8 call for independent selection and duplicate-identity/disagreement evidence. P1 S1.3 also includes principal and compatible supporters, and its AC-03 obligations extend beyond this first fixture. Product eligibility, source independence, trust, principal/hysteresis, weighting, freshness, clock effects, tie behavior and production common-point interpretation remain open within their existing owners. No fixture result adopts a product policy or authorizes a single-peer fallback.

The reviewed first fixture is pure and inert: a batch of 0..=16 intervals with distinct, nonzero caller-asserted labels, exact i64 integer endpoints, and **closed intervals on a continuous real axis**. These synthetic labels are not authenticated source identities; the axis is not a production clock frame. Define global maximum depth D and compute `D > floor(n/2)` independently of an explicitly checked minimum in 1..=16. Empty, insufficient population, no majority, insufficient agreement, multiple maximum components and unique witnessed component have the reviewed priority. If there is one maximum component, return its left endpoint as common witness and its complete sorted supporters and intersection; after the majority and minimum gates, multiple components produce an unsupported *fixture* ambiguity with no chosen witness. This does not settle product tie handling, no-majority behavior or source availability.

## Independent expected facts

The following are manually derived fixture expectations, not outputs calculated from the planned implementation or from Chrony:

- For A=[0,10], B=[1,2], C=[4,5], D=2. Minimum 3 gives insufficient agreement; minimum 2 has two maximum components [1,2] and [4,5] and gives fixture ambiguity. There is no common three-source point.
- For A=[0,0], B=[0,1], C=[1,1], D=2 at two separated singleton components [0,0] and [1,1]. The real open span (0,1) has depth 1, so adjacent integer endpoints do not connect the components.
- For A=[0,2], B=[0,4], C=[2,4], all three contain x=2; the unique maximum is [2,2], witnessed by 2 with supporters A/B/C. Closed touching intervals [−2,0] and [0,7] likewise share the singleton 0.
- Two disjoint groups of two among four have D=2: minimum 2 is met, but strict majority is false. With two overlapping inputs and minimum 3, report actual D=2 and majority=true before returning insufficient population; never lower the minimum.
- Valid singleton and i64-extreme endpoints require exact ordering without endpoint subtraction, increment or midpoint conversion. Duplicate labels reject as a second vote; capacity 17 rejects, with 15/16 as valid neighbors. Input permutations preserve valid outputs and sorted supporters.

The full independent case table, construction review and associated finite resource analysis are identified below. They cover healthy controls and recovery as well as invalid inputs. No product/source behavior is established by these expectations alone.

## Construction and source provenance

The reviewed construction design proposes unavoidable private fallible garde-backed validation of the raw nonzero label, the **whole** `(lo, hi)` pair with a real `lo <= hi` predicate, and the independent minimum. It proposes length-first capacity admission, rejection at the first later duplicate-label index, immutable admitted values, private result construction, typed bounded errors and checked output association. No no-op pair validation, `allow_unvalidated`, unowned skipped field, unchecked public constructor, mutable bypass or optional caller validation is accepted. Valid neighboring controls, direct external bypass checks and actual graph/derive compilation are required. The length-first contract applies at the real batch receiver; ordinary slice error assertions alone do not prove that no element was read. A suitable shared observer seam must be reviewed and tested, or that observation remains a gap.

The integrated inert owner is `crates/time-core/src/selection.rs`, with `crates/time-core/src/lib.rs` module/re-export wiring and external `tests/selection.rs`. At task creation the algorithm was not yet implemented; the later records retain its implementation and qualification. The selected upstream material is Chrony 4.9 `sources.c` at revision `8df4f1263e206585e6c4e61352d042c2dd12916a`: `compare_sort_elements` 698–715, endpoint-tag context 159–168, and `SRC_SelectSource` endpoint/depth/strict-majority ranges 1157–1171, 1204–1205, 1234–1272 and 1279–1280. Retained 60,639-byte source SHA-256 is `019021c49f6ccc545fc78513494136cc4edd015e757b9dee4cd811b6d9b8759d`, Git blob `3c6de32cf546e5df49f45a8076da6049bddad15f`. The source's complete original GPL-2.0-only notice names Richard P. Curnow, 1997–2003, and Miroslav Lichvar, 2011–2016, 2018, 2020–2025; retain the entire grant, disclaimer and distribution notice and actual modification attribution in any derived source, alongside COPYING and corresponding-source/build obligations.

The private pretranslation ledger and provenance record deliberately distinguish the Chrony envelope/containment membership and survivor-minimum behavior from this fixture's unique real-component/common-witness restriction. Header, one NTP caller, upstream unit source and a pinned ntpd-rs alternative have been reviewed as context only; no alternative code or dependency is adopted. Source review does not establish upstream parity, an upstream bug, product policy or licensing clearance for the actual graph. Before translation or integration, reconcile the reviewed records against the actual main candidate, preserve all prior ledger entries, independently review any changed mapping/bytes and admit the actual feature/target/licence graph.

## Planned test path and remaining gates

The original external test draft had four bounded scopes: raw interval admission, raw minimum admission, batch rejection/recovery, and 20 fixed evaluator cases plus 24 three-label permutations, 16 rotations and one healthy bridge neighbor. Its original full recipe was **445 interval, 67 minimum, 68 batch and 62 selection attempts**. Those counts identify the historical authored draft; the current main-source recipe is recorded below. Calls are precharged in the test's per-scope counters; reserve the entire campaign, recovery, validation/formatting, compiler and missing/partial outcomes separately. A best-effort Drop receipt or a post-run counter is not global pre-effect enforcement; missing evidence is not zero work.

The retained first scaffold returned `Unimplemented` from its constructors and evaluator. At that initial checkpoint, no tests had executed. Interval/minimum assertions can demonstrate reached constructor behavior; batch and evaluator assertions cannot reach their owners while required constructors reject, and must be reported as **blocked at prerequisites**, not behavioral RED for unreached logic. The executed constructor stage is recorded below. A legitimate checked construction path or independently admitted seam is required before claiming reached evaluator RED; do not fabricate valid domain values to force it.

The main scaffold integration is now complete. Obtain actual candidate-bound constructor RED and healthy controls under a separate focused admission, then implement checked construction before claiming a reached batch/evaluator RED. Preserve source and contract review, GREEN, applicable format/compiler/Clippy, coverage/mutation and other admitted checks; retain real counts, failures and raw receipts. Independently test output privacy, actual receiver no-read behavior, failure atomicity and useful recovery. Final graph/licence inventory, six-owner analysis, P1/AC-03, native Windows and release qualification remain pending.

## Earlier reviewed prospective test gaps

The retained private draft and historical hashes above are unchanged. A subsequent source-only review identified these additional discriminating cases. They had not been authored or executed at that review checkpoint; the later main-source additions are recorded below:

- Duplicate order `[A,B,B,A]` must reject at later index 2. A scan that instead chooses the first original identity could incorrectly report index 3; keep a healthy distinct-label neighbor.
- Distinct labels A and B with equal intervals A=B=[0,10], plus C=[1,1], D=[4,4], E=[7,7], minimum 3 have n=5, D=3 and exactly three maximum components. Both agreement flags are true, so the fixture ambiguity must report count 3 without choosing a witness.
- Sixteen distinct labels i+1 with intervals `[i,31−i]` for i=0..15, minimum 16, supply 32 distinct endpoint keys and have sole maximum component [15,16], witness 15 and all 16 sorted supporters. This exercises full endpoint capacity and minimum equality.
- With `(label,lo,hi)` values `(u64::MAX,0,7)`, `(2,4,10)`, `(7,−10,−5)`, `(4,1,8)` and minimum 3, the sole maximum component and intersection are [4,7], witness 4, with sorted supporters `[2,4,u64::MAX]`. The excluded label 7 does not vote at the witness.
- One interval `[i64::MIN,i64::MAX]` with minimum 1 has that full unique component, witness `i64::MIN` and its one supporter. This is fixture arithmetic only.

Also add raw rejection and valid-neighbor controls for reversed extreme endpoints `(i64::MAX,i64::MIN)` and minimum `usize::MAX`. Exercise private fields, unchecked/default/conversion/serde and mutation bypass attempts through external compile-fail consumers paired with a compiling legal consumer; check bounded redacted error formatting. The length-17 error test alone cannot prove the real receiver reads or copies no element: use the reviewed shared receiver/trap and valid in-range control, or retain that evidence gap. The scaffold's private fixed supporter array also needs an explicit initialization, prefix-length and padding invariant so no unfilled slot can be exposed; reconcile its representation with the reviewed construction design before treating the implementation as checked.

These four new evaluator fixtures would bring the draft's literal/permutation scope from 61 to **65 selector calls**, above its current local ceiling of 64. Before authoring or running them, review the changed recipes and counter ceilings and reserve the **complete** revised scope, including duplicate/extreme cases, any new privacy and receiver controls, recovery calls and missing/partial outcomes. The earlier 445/67/68/62 counts and hashes remain historical facts about the earlier draft, not a reservation or result for an expanded suite.

## Next bounded slice: fixture anchor projection

The existing plan §4.1 and architecture §6/§7.2.1 require common-time projection,
original observation identity/age and distinct uncertainty terms before dependent
selection/discipline integration. This follow-up remains pure numerical fixture
work under CHECK-7/8/14/25. It adopts no production freshness lifetime, confidence
constant, source-quality rule, quorum or automatic correction authority. OPEN-5/6
do not block this explicitly synthetic slice; they still block their dependent
production policies.

Use one borrowed `FixtureHistory` to obtain both the actual held anchor row and
its current committed report/rate. No independent observation/report pairing or
automatic best-anchor selection is accepted. Retain complete asserted context,
anchor identity/time, latest accepted time, fit reference, projection instant
and exact elapsed durations separately. Projection creates no fresh observation
and cannot mutate history. Current-context mismatch, absent report, unavailable
anchor and backward time need explicit ordered typed rejection with no output.

Phase is **Q33**, not nanoseconds: reuse the checked observation's exact
numerator/`2^33` conversion. Subtract monotonic u64 readings before conversion;
reuse the inclusive `2^53` integer envelope and a positive `/1e9` conversion.
The normalizer's `relative_seconds` deliberately negates elapsed time and cannot
be reused unchanged. That numerical envelope is no freshness policy. Narrow
internal visibility may share actual checked conversion/arithmetic owners.

Explicitly asserted half-delay and dispersion are seconds; asserted skew is
seconds/second. Their fallible private construction must invoke shared garde
rules, reject nonfinite/subnormal/negative values, and specify signed-zero
handling. Keep supplied terms and computed growth distinct. Sequential checked
operations compute anchor phase plus fitted rate times anchor age, and
half-delay plus dispersion plus age times skew. Reject an invalid intermediate
before a later sum can hide it. Immutable output exposes fixture center and
numeric distance terms; it supplies no physical confidence, integer interval
enclosure, production eligibility or Tracking claim.

Independent expected fixtures, before execution:

- Times 98/99/100 seconds with phases `3/32`, `7/64`, `1/8` seconds yield rate
  `1/64`. At 102 seconds, either anchor 98 or 100 projects center `5/32`.
  With half-delay `1/64`, dispersion `1/64`, skew `1/128`, age 2 gives distance
  `3/64`; age 4 gives **`4/64`**, correcting an earlier planning arithmetic slip.
- Equal-weight phases `[1,0,1]` at relative times `[-2,-1,0]` have zero rate;
  latest-anchor projection is 1 while the fitted intercept is analytically 2/3.
  The fitted variance supplies no uncertainty term.
- Near-`u64::MAX` and `2^63` readings preserve one-nanosecond exact differences;
  challenge elapsed `2^53-1`, `2^53`, `2^53+1`, wrong contexts, missing/evicted
  anchors, absent reports, reversed time, repeated projection and negative rate.
- Minimum-normal asserted skew times a small positive age must reject a subnormal
  intermediate even when adding it to a normal base distance could conceal it.

Before native execution, freeze the API/error order/zero policy, actual module
and fixture sources, critical/security registration, complete source graph and
new package test/counter recipes. Discover the actual seventh-owner inventory.
If its union exceeds 512, use explicit disjoint focused cohorts and an exact
same-candidate union admission/checker; no file is dropped and no aggregate score
substitutes for per-file 95%. Reserve cumulative cohort work, baselines and
remaining checks before effects. Historical 501/83-input receipts stay historical
after the integrated candidate changes. The implementation and new qualification
are not yet performed.

The implementation API is now frozen for authoring (reviewed independently,
not yet executed): `FixtureProjectionTerms::new(half_delay_seconds,
dispersion_seconds, skew_seconds_per_second)` privately stores three validated
terms. Finite normal nonnegative values and both signed zeros are accepted;
supplied zero bits are preserved. Nonfinite, subnormal and negative values reject
in declared field order. Integer-duration zero converts to positive zero; other
arithmetic zero bits follow the separately rounded IEEE operation order.

`project_fixture_anchor(&FixtureHistory, expected_context, anchor_index,
expected_anchor_at, at, terms)` returns a private immutable
`FixtureAnchorProjection`. Exact held-row time must match the mandatory
`expected_anchor_at`: an old index after eviction cannot silently select a new
observation. Error precedence is context mismatch, missing report, missing

## Proposed follow-up after projection qualification

A fresh-context read-only planning agent identified the next numerical seam:
the computed binary64 center/distance has no checked integer enclosure consumed
by `FixtureInterval`. After the current candidate is qualified or causally
repaired, independently review a small bridge owner under this same task and
REQ-10/DEC-8, CHECK-7/8/14/25 and the bounded fixture subset of AC-03. Principal
selection, hysteresis and physical confidence policy remain later work.

Proposed `enclose_fixture_projection(&FixtureAnchorProjection)` privately retains
the actual original projection and derives a source-labelled interval, with no
independently supplied label/center/distance. In explicit fixture Q33 units:

```text
lower = floor((exact represented center - exact represented distance) * 2^33)
upper = ceil ((exact represented center + exact represented distance) * 2^33)
```

Both endpoints must fit i64 before immutable publication. Do not first perform
rounded binary64 center±distance: center 1 with distance `2^-80` must enclose to
`[2^33-1, 2^33+1]`, while ordinary binary64 endpoint arithmetic loses that distance.
A genuine constant-phase history at 0/1/2 seconds supplies this proposed public
control with `H=2^-80,D=S=0` and evaluation at its latest row. The existing linear
fixtures supply `[7*2^27,13*2^27]` and `[6*2^27,14*2^27]`. These are independent
analytic facts, not executed enclosure observations.

Retain the quantum and separate lower/upper Exact or OutwardFraction metadata:
each directed slack is zero or strictly between zero and one `2^-33` second tick.
Outward rounding can make previously disjoint sub-tick intervals overlap; claim
only enclosure of the computed fixture operands. Its numerical range is no
OD-02 recovery bound. A subsequent batch consumer must separately enforce common
evaluation instant/frame/generation and preserve original observation ages.

Raw finite-center/nonnegative-distance helper tests may cover exact i64 limits,
fractional signs and overflow without forging a checked projection. A proposed
public extreme fixture with only 3,906,250 ns span was rejected during source
review: it violates the existing 62,500,000 ns minimum before fitting. It is not
an admitted public path or behavioral result. No enclosure source, dependency,
policy, constructor or qualification is adopted by this proposal.

### Enclosure API and arithmetic freeze for authoring

Two supplied-context read-only agents independently reviewed ownership and exact
arithmetic. No mathematical counterexample was found; these are design results,
not executed enclosure observations. Q33 remains a routine explicit fixture
representation. Author one root bridge module; regression must not depend on
selection. The public function is:

```rust
enclose_fixture_projection(&FixtureAnchorProjection)
    -> Result<FixtureProjectedInterval, FixtureEnclosureError>
```

The wrapper privately retains the original projection by value, checked
`FixtureInterval`, and lower/upper `FixtureEndpointRounding::{Exact,
OutwardFraction}`. Accessors return immutable projection/interval references,
copied rounding enums and exact quantum seconds `2^-33`. Derive the label only
from `projection.context().source_id().get()`, then call `FixtureInterval::new`;
preserve its actual error as `FixtureEnclosureError::Interval { source }`.
No independent raw label/scalars, mutable access, Default, unrestricted From or
deserialization is admitted. Copy/Clone preserve invariants; Debug is redacted.
No refit, mutation, observation refresh, ambient read or authority claim occurs.

`Unrepresentable` means no ordered Q33 i64 enclosure is admitted within the
numeric domain: nonfinite values, negative distance or range failure. Promise
no endpoint error priority. Both signed zeros are accepted. Use a separate
bounded `Invariant` for impossible checked internal representation failures.
A private raw-scalar constructor invokes shared garde rules; following helpers
consume checked representations. Necessary prebounds `-2^30 <= c < 2^30` and
`0 <= r < 2^30` do not replace exact endpoint checks. Publish only after both
completed integers pass checked i64 conversion.

The reviewed constant-work algorithm uses sign-magnitude parts:

- Scale normal IEEE exponent field E as `significand * 2^(E-1042)`. For E=0,
  use the mantissa without a hidden bit times `2^-1041`; only zero mantissa is
  zero. This supports raw subnormal helper inputs without changing projection
  or terms admission. Proper fractions have at most 53 numerator bits and
  shift at most 1041. Do not normalize tiny negative operands into a floor plus
  an enormous positive fraction.
- Compare nonzero fractions by `bit_length(numerator)-shift`. Equal leading
  powers permit denominator alignment; aligned numerators still have at most
  53 bits. Treat zero explicitly; checked u64 or u128 suffices.
- Two magnitude fractions below one half sum below one. Otherwise choose the
  larger fraction at least one half: its shift is at most 53, so its complement
  to one is bounded exactly. Compare the other fraction to that complement to
  distinguish no carry, exact carry one, and carry with nonzero residual.
- Combine signed wholes/carry in checked i128. Opposite-sign fractions need
  only ordering. Floor subtracts one for a negative residual; ceil adds one for
  a positive residual. Residual zero alone means Exact, including cancellation
  or an exact sum of two nonzero fractions.

Additional frozen analytic raw-helper facts use Q=`2^33`, E/O for Exact or
OutwardFraction; they are not executed or asserted public-history paths:

| Center | Distance | Bounds | Lower/upper |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| either zero sign | either zero sign | `[0,0]` | E/E |
| `2^-1022` or `2^-1074` | 0 | `[0,1]` | O/O |
| negative of either preceding center | 0 | `[-1,0]` | O/O |
| 1 | `2^-80` or `2^-1074` | `[Q-1,Q+1]` | O/O |
| `2^-34` | `2^-34` | `[0,1]` | E/E |
| `2^-35` | `3*2^-35` | `[-1,1]` | O/E |
| `-2^-35` | `3*2^-35` | `[-1,1]` | E/O |
| `3*2^-35` | `3*2^-35` | `[0,2]` | E/O |
| `1+2^-34` | `2^-34` | `[Q,Q+1]` | E/E |
| `-2^30` | 0 | `[i64::MIN,i64::MIN]` | E/E |
| `-2^30` | any positive tiny distance | Unrepresentable | — |
| `2^30-2^-23` | `1023*2^-33` | `[2^63-2047,i64::MAX]` | E/E |
| same preceding center | `2047*2^-34` | Unrepresentable | — |
| `-2^29` | `2^29` | `[i64::MIN,0]` | E/E |
| `+2^29` | `2^29` | Unrepresentable | — |

Public controls use genuine checked histories/projections and preserve every
original field/bit. Cover tiny-distance cancellation, linear dyadic facts,
oversized asserted distance, repeated reads and sub-tick disjoint inputs whose
outward intervals overlap without physical-agreement claims. Raw helper tests
must not forge a checked projection. Extend compiler consumers with the five
existing projection probes and new-wrapper private/mutation/default/raw-conversion/
raw-receiver cases, paired with genuine supported producer-to-bridge behavior.
Freeze actual source/critical/security inventory, test/counter recipes and one
complete cumulative campaign before execution. The 86-input checkpoint becomes
historical when product source changes.

Compatibility is coupled and additive: existing history/projection and generic
selection APIs retain their contracts; the new bridge is their explicit new
consumer. No serialized DTO, persisted record, independently versioned endpoint
or surviving cross-process fixture changes, so no migration or old-reader
retirement is performed. Generic batches still lack projection-context checks;
extracting an interval cannot establish those checks or physical agreement.
The raw helper's bit-exact support for finite subnormals changes neither fit nor
projection/terms admission. New compiler rejection capacity is explicitly
reviewed as 106 = 93 prior + five existing projection + eight wrapper probes;
legal consumers remain ten, and native counts stay pending until executed.

### Proposed subsequent compatibility consumer

After current qualification, a bounded next fixture slice can retain a borrowed
`&[FixtureProjectedInterval]` in a private `FixtureProjectedBatch<'a>`, with an
explicit expected nonzero frame, nonzero mapping generation and `MonotonicNs`
evaluation instant. These are caller assertions, not actual clock-origin,
current-authority or source-independence evidence. Retain every original wrapper
and its ages; copy only checked intervals into the existing batch owner.

Freeze rejection order before authoring: capacity above sixteen before entry
reads; then ascending index, frame before mapping generation before exact
evaluation time; then necessarily call actual `FixtureBatch::new` and preserve
its `BatchError`. Thus `[A,A,B_wrong_frame]` reports index-two context failure,
while compatible `[A,B,A,B]` delegates the first later duplicate at index two.
Never compare whole source contexts, drop entries or reproject a mismatch.
Mandatory contextual garde validation inside the constructor owns the new
relationships, with one shared ordered predicate and no unvalidated fields.
Expected scalar shape remains owned by existing checked types. An immutable
typed selection method can delegate to `select_fixture` with the unchanged
`FixtureMinimum`; its detached numerical result creates no production authority.

Independent genuine-history facts, with `T=2^27` Q33 units, frame 43, mapping
generation 47 and evaluation at 102 seconds: source 41's existing linear history
anchored at 100 seconds encloses `[7T,13T]`; source 53's same numerical history
anchored at 98 seconds encloses `[6T,14T]`; source 59's constant one-second phase
with `H=1/64,D=S=0` encloses `[63T,65T]`. Population three/minimum two has depth
two, one witnessed intersection `[7T,13T]`, witness `7T`, supporters `[41,53]`.
Minimum three instead gives insufficient agreement; the first and third sources
alone with minimum two give no majority. Preserve anchor ages two/four seconds
and latest-observation age two seconds. These are planned expectations only.

Use constant one-eighth-second histories for same-number context-fault controls
and 0/15/16/17 capacity neighbors; radius `1/64` gives `[7T,9T]` independently.
Sixteen distinct labels and minimum sixteen produce the existing selector's
depth-sixteen witness. Compare complete wrapper bit snapshots, permutations and
repeated selections; include a one-nanosecond evaluation mismatch with identical
numbers. External bypass/borrow-lifetime diagnostics need genuine legal-use
neighbors. Constructor and garde-wiring guards need direct evidence even where
the mutation tool generates no constructor fault. This proposed slice advances
bounded CHECK-7/8/14/25 without adopting eligibility, freshness, hysteresis,
physical-confidence, trust or combination policy and completes neither S1.3 nor P1.

## Combination prerequisites and kernel, 31 October 2026

The full P0–P7 goal remains active. This next S1.3 step connects the actual
principal/projection outputs to the full numerical combination outputs. Its
kernel is an aligned mathematical component; compatibility penalties, event
ownership and production admission remain required subsequent integration.

### Kernel contract for translation

Requirements: architecture §7.2(3,6), REQ-10/DEC-8 and CHECK-7/8/14/25. The owner
will be `crates/time-core/src/combination.rs`, with only module/re-export wiring
in `src/lib.rs`. Keep the checked square-root primitive crate-internal in the
existing regression arithmetic owner; its three actual uncertainty outputs are
the intended non-test consumers. No public low-level math API, lint suppression,
discarded square root, dependency or build-setting change is authorized by this
contract.

The public fixture-kernel entry consumes an actual `FixturePrincipalDecision`
and at most sixteen checked uncertainty records. Each record borrows one actual
original decision entry and supplies explicit phase standard deviation in seconds,
rate standard deviation in seconds/second and tracking skew in seconds/second,
all asserted at that entry's common projection instant. Tracking skew is explicit
and distinct from the projection's supplied growth term; neither is inferred
from residual diagnostics or claimed to be physically calibrated. Mandatory
Garde-backed construction admits finite normal-or-signed-zero nonnegative raw
uncertainties, preserving bits. No optional caller validation or raw public fields.

Capacity precedes indexed work. The receiver requires exactly one record for
every actual witnessed supporter, no extras/duplicates, and the actual original
entry identity, common frame/generation/evaluation time and principal membership.
It never accepts a caller-defined naked-label subset. Fixed arrays and at most
sixteen-by-sixteen searches bound storage/work. Use the actual witness's ascending
label order for deterministic unfused reductions. An absent principal returns an
explicit unavailable kernel outcome and requires empty metadata; the original
principal outcome kind is preserved. A retained principal can yield an inert
numerical view; the kernel creates no event credit, publication or clock authority.

For a genuine singleton, copy all five original principal/uncertainty quantities
exactly after metadata admission, without reciprocals or square roots. This is
not a fallback after lost agreement or an invalid participant. For two or more
supporters, root distance, rate SD and tracking skew must be strictly positive
normal before reciprocals. Phase SD may be zero. Zero denominators, unsupported
intermediate magnitudes, square overflow/subnormal/nonzero-to-zero underflow and
nonfinite results reject atomically, with a typed fixed stage/step/cause. Never
clamp, drop a participant or silently return a singleton.

For each supporter i, freeze `a_i = 1 / root_distance_i` and
`b_i = 1 / rate_sd_i²`. Fold original positive-behind phase and its signed
seconds/second fitted rate. Preserve the pinned primitive order: square each
phase SD and each difference from the ORIGINAL principal phase, add those
squares, then multiply by phase weight. Outputs are phase `Σ(a_i phase_i)/Σa_i`,
phase SD `sqrt(Σ[a_i(phase_sd_i² + (phase_i-principal_phase)²)]/Σa_i)`,
rate `Σ(b_i rate_i)/Σb_i`, rate SD `1/sqrt(Σb_i)` and tracking skew
`1/sqrt(Σ[1/tracking_skew_i²])`. Retain count/labels and original principal-kind
provenance, without a claimed independent-sample confidence gain.

Independent two-source facts: genuine latest-anchor, common-instant histories
A `(phase=0,rate=0,distance=1,phaseSD=1,rateSD=1,skew=1)` and B
`(phase=3*2^-10,rate=5*2^-20,distance=2,phaseSD=1,rateSD=1/2,skew=1)`
with principal A give phase `2^-10`, rate `2^-18`, phase SD
`sqrt(1+3*2^-20)`, rate SD `1/sqrt(5)` and skew `1/sqrt(2)`. B's exact
three phases `(3062,3067,3072)*2^-20` at 0/1/2 seconds supply its actual
fitted rate. Use anchor index 2 at evaluation 2 seconds; oldest-anchor growth
with skew one would invalidate the declared distances. These are analytic facts,
not yet executed kernel evidence. Include singleton, absent agreement, metadata
bypass, wrong association, zero reciprocals and finite overflow/underflow cases
with accepted neighboring controls.

The pinned Chrony mapping selects only singleton/init, reciprocal weights,
principal-centered reductions, complete outputs and count. It intentionally does
not import tracking production, extra time extrapolation, compatibility/distant
penalty state, status mutation, logging or reference effects. Existing checked
projections supply common-time phase/rate. Before production combination, add a
transactional owner for actual compatibility limits, startup/mature penalties,
re-entry, principal changes and replay-safe reference credit; this kernel alone
discharges none of those obligations. Preserve minimum agreement and the held
production vote-population, freshness and uncertainty decisions.

### Prerequisite execution evidence

The main checkout now retains the exact rate already used by projection, with a
private field and immutable `phase_rate_seconds_per_second()` getter. No new
production fit, history read or arithmetic was added. Complete snapshots in the
projection/enclosure/projected-batch tests include the new field; their original
numerical assertions retain their meanings. Source review derived getter deltas
+66/+47/+121 respectively. Projection tests add one real append and one
projection; old snapshots retain their fitted rate. The independent four-point

nonnegative finite max-clock-error are fixture inputs, not shipped defaults or
production configuration decisions. Production eligibility, calibration,
acquisition/incarnation/reset and vote-population choices remain held.

Requirements are architecture 7.2(3,6)/7.2.1, REQ-10, DEC-8 and
CHECK-7/8/14/25. Source owner remains `time-core`: a private `combination`
lifecycle child and a private admitted-record/contributor-mask bridge reuse the
actual principal transition and existing checked numerical sums. Existing public
all-witness kernel behavior remains unchanged. New state is fixed-roster and
fixed-capacity, stages its complete inner state, validates complete metadata
before filtering and commits only after successful output completion. Only ready
transitions emit a new fixture estimate/ordinal. Reachability maturity counts
both successful and failed explicit ordinal events, saturating at eight; source
penalties retain startup-one/mature-32 reset/countdown and upstream ordering.

Two reviewed test qualifications apply: practical public trajectories cannot
reach u64 ordinal overflow from zero with exact-next admission; exercise the
actual private checked-increment seam and label that narrower evidence. Several
internal root/output failures are unreachable through genuine checked producers;
test reachable denominator/compatibility errors, root success and public
rollback/retry rather than forging invalid checked state or masks.

Independent `/root/lifecycle_fixture_design` supplied six genuine-producer
trajectories: strict distance/rate thresholds and original-principal comparison;
startup/replay/supporter-only pauses; mixed reachability/mature-32 countdown and
relapse; metadata/overflow rejection with corrected same-event retry and distance
short circuit; true witness-two/contributor-one versus genuine singleton; and
outside-witness reset, loss, waiting and genuine reacquisition. Multi-round
fixtures retain independent per-source histories and change one latest
observation at each common evaluation instant, preserving existing receiver
admission. Outcomes expose original witness/minimum separately from contributors.

Execution ownership: Root owns the parent bridge, exports, all compiler/Python/
test/format/tool admission and integration. One worker owns only the new
lifecycle child; another owns only the new integration-test source. No native
campaign or candidate freeze occurs until the coherent behavior, source-owned
development budget and focused cases are complete. Use focused development
execution first, then the required gates on the stable candidate. No dependency,
network, clock, service, persistence or Windows authority is introduced.

## S152 same-owner fitted tracking journey — frozen authoring contract

3 November 2026. This is the next coherent pure Core fixture in the existing
S1.3/P1 chain, linked to [S151 same-fit standard errors](s1.2-regression-readiness.md#s151-selected-linux-fixture-gates-and-retained-receiving--complete).
Authority: architecture §7.2.1, §17 and §20.1; REQ-8/10/14,
CHECK-7/8/19/20/25 and CHECK-14 supplied-age semantics only. Source owner is
`time-core`; environment is admitted Linux Rust, controlled synthetic inputs.
No live network, production eligibility, clock, native Windows or release claim.
Windows execution, the separately held fuzz runner and storage experiments stay
held. This record precedes translation; planned expectations are not results.

### Ownership, API and compatibility

Add `project_fixture_adaptive_tracking(history, expected_context, anchor_index,
expected_anchor_at, at, projection_terms, tracking_skew_seconds_per_second)` over
the genuine borrowed `FixtureAdaptiveHistory`, returning the existing
`FixtureAnchorProjection` or additive `FixtureTrackingProjectionError`.
Add immutable `FixtureAnchorProjection::tracking() -> Option<&FixtureTrackingEstimate>`.
The opaque payload retains four private checked f64s, with immutable getters
`phase_seconds`, `phase_standard_deviation_seconds`,
`rate_standard_deviation_seconds_per_second`, `tracking_skew_seconds_per_second`.
Whole-value Copy/Clone and redacted Debug are allowed. No public constructor,
Default, Serde, mutation, attachment override or rebinding API. Context, rate,
anchor/latest/reference/evaluation instants belong to the enclosing projection;
the payload does not retain sample count or degrees of freedom.

Old projection producers publish None. Only the genuine same-owner new producer
publishes Some. Existing enclosure copies the whole admitted projection unchanged.
This attachment is minted before copies; combination bindings are made after
entries occupy the actual final contiguous batch. Matching context/times never
substitutes for the actual producing report. Projection has no upward dependency
on principal or combination modules. No serialized/stored independently versioned
reader exists at this internal fixture boundary; no data migration is introduced.

Add explicit `combine_fixture_tracking_supporters(&FixturePrincipalDecision)`
and `FixtureCombinationState::advance_tracking(&FixtureProjectedBatch, strata)`.
The new kernel accepts no caller uncertainty array, detached report, freely paired
metadata or override. Legacy kernel/lifecycle APIs retain anchor phase and supplied
uncertainties even when their projection carries tracking. Interval majority,
root distance, principal scoring, provenance and observation watermarks remain
anchor-based; neither API adopts a new product policy or resets lifecycle state.

### Producer order and actual failure owners

1. Existing adaptive association: complete context, committed report, fitted
   anchor index, exact anchor time and reference association.
2. Execute the complete original raw-anchor projection without arithmetic changes:
   backward guard, exact checked durations/conversions and its five primitives.
   These failures precede any new skew failure.
3. Mandatory shared Garde admission of the separately supplied tracking skew:
   finite normal or signed zero, nonnegative.
4. Call standard_errors() on that exact selected report; retain S151's ten-step
   arithmetic order and typed cause. Do not fit or scan history again.
5. Compute Δ = at minus the own fit_reference, using checked monotonic duration
   and the existing exact 2^53-nanosecond conversion owner. Reuse Domain/Conversion
   errors through FixtureProjectionError and the new Projection wrapper.
6. Four separate checked Response-stage primitives in this order:
   rate * Δ; fitted_intercept + shift; rate_SD * Δ; intercept_SD + growth.
7. Mandatory final scalar admission in phase, phase_SD, rate_SD, tracking_skew
   order, followed by atomic publication of projection plus complete attachment.

Use fit-reference age, not anchor age; rate SD grows phase SD, not tracking skew.
Preserve rate bits from the same enclosing projection. No fusion, reassociation,
clamp, floor, confidence multiplier, fallback or extra physical interpretation.
Reuse the existing finite/subnormal/underflow policy and shared scalar predicates.
New closed types are FixtureTrackingField (four scalar roles), FixtureTrackingStep
(four primitives), and FixtureTrackingProjectionError with Projection{source},
StandardErrors{source}, InvalidScalar{field,issue}, Arithmetic{step,source},
ValidationInvariant. Preserve existing exhaustive public error enums unchanged.
All new helpers have explicit input/output/error/effect/lifecycle obligations;
Error::source preserves actual nested causes and messages are bounded/redacted.

### Complete-witness receiving and lifecycle

A private capacity-16 holder owns checked uncertainty values plus fitted phase,
borrowing the decision's actual contiguous batch slots through the existing
FixtureSupporterUncertainty relation. A closed private view represents legacy
borrowed records or derived tracking records. No Vec, unsafe flattening or
self-reference. Retain existing pointer identity, membership, duplicate, context,
evaluation, capacity and count guards. Preserve direct legacy private controls
through narrow wrappers where needed.

Require tracking on every original canonical witness participant before any
compatibility filter, including later-excluded entries and retained/no-new-estimate
decisions. Untracked nonwitness entries remain valid for unchanged selection.
MissingTracking{index} means the ascending canonical witness ordinal, 0..15;
reject without dropping a vote, substituting a scalar or falling back to legacy.
Absent-principal unavailable/waiting behavior remains unchanged. Additive
FixtureTrackingCombinationError wraps MissingTracking or Combination{source};
FixtureTrackingLifecycleError wraps MissingTracking or Lifecycle{source}.

One consistent phase mode governs contribution sums, original-principal-centered
disagreement, singleton copying, admission and filtered lifecycle reduction.
A genuine original singleton copies all five admitted scalars including zero
SD/skew. Multisource contributors retain positive-denominator checks; an original
witness of two filtered to one still follows the multisource path. No requirement
that fitted phase lie inside the anchor-interval intersection is introduced.

Lifecycle stages the actual principal transition, derives/admit all witness
records, applies existing compatibility and penalties, reduces, checks outcome,
then commits once. Any failure preserves the complete original state. Later
projection alone grants no new source watermark, pending credit or emitted round.
Retained/non-ready evaluation does not run compatibility evaluation or penalty
decrements; existing outside-witness penalty resets are preserved. Successful
retained evaluation may advance evaluated_at. Actual new observations follow
existing pending-credit rules.

### Independent expectations and discriminating controls

Before source authoring, retain exact Fraction/100-digit Decimal facts in
`s152-tracking-independent-facts-v1.json` under the existing external evidence
root. Two genuine adaptive histories at t=0,1,2 have divisors (1,1,4): A phases
(0,1/8,0), B adds 1/16. Evaluate latest anchor at t=4, with explicit projection
terms (half-delay=1/4, dispersion=0, growth-skew=1/8) and separate tracking-skew=1/8.
Both distances are 1/2; A is the actual asserted/tested principal, not an assumed tie.

| Quantity | Independent expected value |
| --- | --- |
| Fitted A / B phase at t=4 | 7/36 / 37/144 |
| Raw-anchor A / B centers | 1/12 / 7/48 |
| Tracking / legacy combined phase | 65/288 / 11/96 |
| Each aged phase SD | (sqrt(5)+3)/18 |
| Principal-centered disagreement variance | 1/512 (not mean-centered 1/1024) |
| Combined phase SD | sqrt(((sqrt(5)+3)/18)^2 + 1/512) |
| Combined rate / rate SD / supplied-skew reduction | 1/24 / 1/(12 sqrt(2)) / 1/(8 sqrt(2)) |
| Actual Q33 enclosure A | [-3579139414, 5010795179] |
| Actual Q33 enclosure B | [-3042268502, 5547666091] |

Use relative tolerance 2^-38 for nonzero floating expectations, no absolute floor;
known zero and copied scalars require exact bits. The existing post-prune S150
vector has fitted t1..8, auxiliary t0; at t9 its fitted phase is -15/224 versus
raw oldest-fitted-anchor (index0, t1) -499/3584, and aged SD is sqrt(5245/5505024)+sqrt(1049/19267584).

Tests must observe genuine producer→copied enclosure→final contiguous batch→new
kernel/lifecycle, actual principal/contributors, unchanged legacy, post-prune
population, same-context/time different genuine histories, missing mixed tracking
including later-excluded participant, no-principal behavior, projection-only
reevaluation versus real observation credit, singleton zero versus multisource
zero, original-two filtered to one, and failure atomicity. Exercise real public
binding guards where constructible; use external compile-fail plus legal controls
for private attachment/override/lifetime bypasses. Never invent a public mismatch
constructor. Prefer typed equality/Boolean assertions for new failures so valid
Rust multiline assertion output does not trigger another Cargo-JSON transport
mismatch. Preserve strict parser behavior and all earlier failures.

S151 tiny direct FitInput values are not representable adaptive Q33 observations.
The rejected phase(1,2,1)/divisor2^1020 proposal violates genuine observation
bounds and is excluded. Whole-adaptive standard-error failure propagation remains
unexercised unless a valid case is demonstrated; do not forge checked state or
claim private-helper evidence as a public adaptive path. The independent shared
Garde omission/discard sensitivity gap also remains explicit.

## S157 retained-owner chart receiving — authoring contract

3 November 2026. Continue S155/S156 under REQ-8/10/14 and
CHECK-7/8/10/14/25. This is an inert numerical-view continuation on the existing
raw-source owner, not a new target-chart seed or an installed correction.
The whole receiving milestone includes principal and combination lifecycle;
implement the shared principal receiver first, then the combination receiver
with explicit arithmetic-bound propagation. Neither child alone closes S1.3/P1.

The additive principal API is
`FixturePrincipalState::advance_chart(expected_chart, batch, strata)`, returning
a chart-specific immutable decision owning the actual `FixtureChartSelection`
from `batch.select(self.minimum())`. An externally supplied weaker selection is
not accepted. It borrows the actual batch, ordered original chart entries and
their original descriptors; no detached selection pairing, transformed legacy
wrapper or public chart-to-legacy conversion is introduced.

The expected descriptor is an explicit caller assertion for this numerical view:
compare complete checked frames/domain and exact displacement/rate bits, accepting
independently constructed equal descriptors. It is not broker authority. The
chart's source frame/generation must match the retained raw-source state. Target
tags describe only output coordinates; they do not replace observation identity
or imply successor ordering. A later mathematical view may use another explicitly
expected target descriptor over that same retained raw origin. This does not
install a source-generation transition or acknowledge an achieved clock effect.

Reuse one original projection metadata adapter and the existing admission,
staging, ranking and credit transition. Ranking uses original root distance;
chart roundoff widens geometry and remains separately observable, without
becoming statistical SD, source quality or new observation credit. Preserve
configured minimum, original observation timestamps/ages, scores, pending flags,
cached absent-source metadata and failure atomicity. Evaluation time may advance;
unchanged original watermarks cannot become a new update. Do not skip existing
selection/ranking or consumption of already pending credit. Existing unavailable,
waiting and outside-witness behavior retains its defined semantics.

Independent principal exit cases: an existing legacy-ready owner accepts a
nonzero phase/rate chart without reseeding; unchanged observations retain the
principal with no new estimate; equal descriptors at different addresses retain
actual batch/entry pointers; another expected mathematical target view preserves
raw owner identity/credit; one genuine newer principal observation creates one
ready result and replay creates none. Wrong expected descriptor, wrong raw
origin, backward evaluation/observation, changed stratum without observation and
multiple new observations reject without changing the owner; valid neighboring
retry matches an untouched control. Unavailable agreement and preserved pending
credit remain covered through the shared transition and selected regressions.

Root owns the existing task and integration scenarios; a principal worker owns
the receiver and shared metadata/selection transition. Complementary read-only
reviews map the two receivers; a fresh-context review checks this contract before
implementation. Linux format/compiler/Clippy, ordinary/optimized tests and
doctests use the configured guarded verifier. Focused evidence precedes the next
stable candidate's applicable external contracts, coverage/mutation and mechanism
gates; historical S155 scores do not transfer to changed principal bytes. Keep
one evidence chain and no optional tooling expansion. No Windows run is needed.

Combination remains a required following child: clone the complete lifecycle,
derive tracking from actual chart entries, retain the chart decision through the
result and commit only after all admission/reduction checks. Preserve existing
event/reachability watermarks, pending credit, penalties and emission ordinals.
Freeze deterministic arithmetic-bound propagation before its implementation;
never pair transformed raw-anchor center with fitted SD or silently drop chart
roundoff. The nonzero-residual weighted fixture distinguishes fitted phase
`65/288` from raw mean `11/96`; at query4, reference2, D=`4/1024`, F=`1/1024`,
the transformed fitted mean is `1013/4608`, and rate is `1/24 - 1/1024`.
These are independent rational facts, not outputs from the candidate.

### S157 combination arithmetic — reviewed next-child contract

Complementary source review and a fresh-context mathematical review found the
following companion-error model conditionally ready for authoring, with the
source-fit qualifications below. No Rust implementation, reached bound or
lifecycle qualification is established by this review. Principal qualification
above remains the next execution step; this records the required following child
without another survey or parallel plan.

For the actual contributor mask C of a nonsingleton original witness, interpret
stored original fitted phases P_i, rates R_i, root distances d_i, rate SDs s_i
and chart D/F as exact binary64 real values. Use exact integer elapsed nanoseconds
in `delta = D + F * (t - tref) / 1e9`. The references are
`mean_phase = sum((P_i - delta) / d_i) / sum(1 / d_i)` and
`mean_rate = sum((R_i - F) / s_i^2) / sum(1 / s_i^2)`.
These bounds exclude old fit/projection error and physical uncertainty. The
ideal-fit `1013/4608` fact above is a separate comparison, not the reference for
a claim covering only new arithmetic. Exact containment tests must derive
rationals from the actual original stored input bits.

Carry private point/error companions through the actual existing separately
rounded operations in ascending-label order: reciprocal distance, rate-SD
square and reciprocal, weighted products, sums and final division. Initialize
phase companions from actual chart fitted-phase/error attachments and rates from
actual chart rate/error. Weight with original `source_projection()` distance;
raw-anchor center/error remains selection geometry. A privately constructed
chart-specific combined output must expose separate phase/rate mean-roundoff
bounds and retain actual chart decision/batch provenance. Statistical SD/skew
remain distinct and receive no new arithmetic-bound claim.

For `|A-a| <= Ea`, `|B-b| <= Eb`, let u=`2^-53`, eta be the existing conservative
underflow upper, and U mean outward evaluation of **every** nonnegative primitive:

- Addition/subtraction: `U(Ea + Eb + u*(abs(a)+abs(b)) + eta)`.
- Multiplication: `U(abs(a)*Eb + abs(b)*Ea + Ea*Eb + u*abs(a)*abs(b) + eta)`.
- Division: for `b > 0`, `0 < L <= b-Eb`, `Q >= abs(a)/b`, use
  `U((Ea + Q*Eb)/L + u*Q + eta)`.

One final upward successor after ordinarily evaluating a compound expression
is insufficient. Preserve the actual rounded SD square and its error; do not
substitute a different weight formula. A new private lower-margin primitive must
admit finite `b > 0`, finite `Eb >= 0`, including subnormal bounds, reject
`b <= Eb`, and guarantee finite `0 < L <= b-Eb`. Compute `r = RN(b-Eb)` separately;
a positive subnormal result is exact and may be returned directly. Otherwise
use `r.next_down()` only if finite and strictly positive. A simpler unconditional
successor-down implementation may reject the smallest positive subnormal margin,
but must declare that narrower envelope. Never clamp a lower bound upward or
reuse an upward-bound primitive. Failure/overflow/unproved margin preserves the
whole lifecycle before its sole commit. Existing eta=`MIN_POSITIVE` may reject
some otherwise successful point arithmetic; do not claim unchanged acceptance.

Singleton is determined by the **original witnessed population**, not the final
contributor count: a genuine singleton copies transformed fitted phase/rate and
their errors with denominator-free references `P-delta` and `R-F`; its distance
or rate SD may be zero. A larger witness filtered to one contributor retains
ordinary denominator checks and reduction. Preserve the existing complete
witness metadata admission before filtering and the full lifecycle transaction.

Independent detecting cases for the implementation:

- `b=1, Eb=2^-54`: rounded subtraction is1, but exact margin is smaller;
  `next_down(1)` is a valid lower bound. `b=Eb=1` rejects.
- `b=next_up(MIN_POSITIVE), Eb=MIN_POSITIVE`: exact margin is `2^-1074`;
  no upward normal clamp is permitted. `b=1, Eb=next_down(1)` has margin `2^-53`.
- Sixteen equal stored phase/rate points `0x1.999999999999ap-4`, weights one:
  ascending rounded mean is `0x1.999999999999bp-4`; its error from the exact
  stored-input mean is `2^-56`, larger than an individual identity-chart bound.
  Carrying only per-input chart error therefore fails this reduction control.
- Rate SD `0x1.6666666666666p+0`: rounded square
  `0x1.f5c28f5c28f5bp+0`, reciprocal `0x1.05397829cbc16p-1`; exact reciprocal
  weight error exceeds a reciprocal-only rounding term. Retain square error.
- Genuine singleton, larger witness filtered to one, two/sixteen contributors,
  unequal weights, mixed signs/cancellation, overflow, unavailable margin,
  missing later-excluded tracking, full rollback and healthy retry.

Complete bound/mechanism controls and exact-rational containment of actual Rust
outputs are required, followed by retained-owner same-observation and genuine
new-observation lifecycle tests. No installed source-generation transition,
actual correction, clock acknowledgement or production confidence follows.

### S1.4a reference-input readiness — admitted implementation contract

4 November 2026. First pure S1.4 prerequisite, independently reviewed after the
S158 checkpoint above. Authority: architecture §6, §7.2.1 and §10.1–10.3;
REQ-8/10/11/14/15 and the pure input/lifecycle portions of CHECK-7/8/10/14/25.
The existing `FixtureChartCombinationOutcome` is the real upstream producer.
There is currently no discipline caller; the first consumers are genuine public
fixture tests, and subsequent controller equations must accept the checked input.

Implement one private `reference_input` module in `time-core`, exported through
`lib.rs`. `FixtureReferenceInputState<'chart>` retains an immutable reference to
one explicitly bound `FixtureAffineChart` instance and a readiness enum. This
asserts a modeled fixture view, without proving an installed/current Windows
mapping or continuity across separately constructed owners. Binding the whole
chart instance preserves its source/target frames, generations, domain, phase
and rate; matching labels alone are insufficient. Existing S158 semantic chart
matching remains unchanged.

`admit_reference_input(&self, &genuine_outcome)` must require ReferenceReady,
an actual combined estimate and emitted round, readiness of this owner, and
`ptr::eq` between the combined estimate's actual chart and the bound chart.
One mandatory shared Garde relationship rule and typed checks own this receiving
boundary. Rejection leaves this owner and the upstream outcome unchanged; a
foreign chart gets one closed `ForeignChartView` error, including separately
constructed equal descriptors. Do not add redundant tag guards hidden behind
the exact-instance check.

Return a privately constructed non-Clone `FixtureReferenceInput` borrowing both
owner and actual outcome. Accessors borrow through the token; no `into_inner`,
owned conversion or longer-lived underlying-reference escape is added. A live
token subsequently used by a consumer prevents mutable feedback on its owner.
Reusing an unchanged Ready outcome is read-only reuse of the same original round
and observation metadata; it creates no new observation, proposal or clock effect.

`receive_feedback` accepts only the closed modeled outcomes ProvenNoEffect,
CoordinateChanged and Unknown. Readiness follows the monotone lattice
Ready → CoordinateChanged → Unknown, with Unknown dominating every ordering.
ProvenNoEffect is the identity in every state. All repeats are idempotent;
no feedback can restore readiness. No operation IDs, revisions, pending request,
reservation, budget, settlement ledger or quantitative achieved-effect payload
is introduced. There is no mapping/history transform, restoration, controller
equation, finite phase plan, holdover, leap or OS effect in this prerequisite.
Actual delivered slew intervals, quantization, effect composition and a later
reconciliation/reacquisition contract remain separate dependent work.

Independent expected results and finite development checks:

- One genuine emitted two-source S158 Ready outcome admits with original chart,
  estimate, round and observation metadata. Repeated reads preserve the same
  emitted round and the real upstream owner's observation accounting.
- Genuine WaitingForUpdate, Unavailable and RetainedWithoutNewEstimate reject.
  Foreign equal/different-numeric descriptors and four frame/generation variants
  reject, each beside an unchanged valid original-instance neighbor.
- An explicit feedback truth table covers all nine ordered pairs and repetitions:
  no-effect preserves existing readiness, changed invalidates old input, and
  Unknown remains dominant. Rejection never changes the accepted fixture state.
- A privacy/constructor bypass and a live-token/mutable-feedback borrow bypass
  fail compilation for their intended rules beside a legal scoped-token control.

Owners: one implementation worker owns the new module and `lib.rs`; one
independent test worker owns the shared-fixture integration test extension and
its private test module. Root owns this task, admission/configuration changes and
integrated checks. No dependency, compiler-policy or existing arithmetic change
is authorized by this contract. Reuse the existing genuine fixture helpers and
configured portable runner. Freeze actual test/producer census and finite source
caps before later expensive qualification; focused authoring is not that gate.
The S158 receipts continue to identify their unchanged tested snapshot after new
source is authored. All execution holds and product decision owners remain.

### S1.4b same-reference chart composition — authoring contract

4 November 2026. Next bounded pure numerical prerequisite after S1.4a; no
achieved-effect receipt or controller is introduced. Authority remains
architecture §6, §7.2.1, §10.1–10.3 and §20.1; REQ-8/10/11/14/15 and the
mathematical/provenance portions of CHECK-7/8/10/14/25. Review and authoring use
the repository `plan-architecture`, `implement-rust`, `implement-rust-contracts`
and numerical-kernel guidance. The preceding checkpoint retains its frozen
154-input candidate after this source changes.

Existing flow is immutable history → `prepare_chart` → genuine anchor/fitted
projection → enclosure → chart batch → principal/combination → checked reference
input. A chart subtracts `P + F*(t-r)` from the original phase and `F` from the
original rate, using the same unchanged monotonic variable. This is a phase
displacement model, not general affine substitution of the time coordinate.

The bounded extension composes A→B with B→C only when the intermediate frame
and generation match exactly and both complete domains (start/reference/end)
are identical. Result A→C keeps the original monotonic data and domain. Existing
frame rules reject an endpoint frame or generation cycle; do not invent new
labels or weaken those rules. Domain intersection, reference rebasing, physical
effect admission, delivered active-time intervals and readiness restoration
remain later work.

`FixtureAffineChart` owns the result, with two private nonnegative coefficient
roundoff bounds in addition to its existing phase/rate point values. Direct
`new` treats admitted binary64 coefficients as exact stored-bit inputs and sets
both bounds to zero. A new fallible composition method stages two separately
rounded point sums and conservative outward coefficient-error bounds; it also
accepts already composed operands. Include inherited error from both operands
and the new addition. Check every arithmetic result and bound against the
existing finite-normal-or-zero policy; overflow, unsupported subnormal point
results or invalid outward bounds reject without publishing a partial chart.
One mandatory shared Garde relationship rule owns chain/domain admission;
mandatory checked result construction owns every scalar/bound. No public raw
fields, unchecked constructor, lossy conversion or mutable error reset is added.

`chart/roundoff.rs` must propagate phase-coefficient error and the actual elapsed
magnitude times rate-coefficient error into displacement/phase bounds, and
rate-coefficient error into rate bounds. Preserve the existing evaluation-error
terms and original fitted-versus-anchor distinction. These bounds concern
arithmetic relative to the exact stored-bit composition inputs; they are not
statistical uncertainty, physical confidence or a measured actuator error.
Keep the direct zero-error descriptor's existing point values and bound bits
unchanged. Work and storage stay constant, independent of composition depth.

`chart_batch.rs` owns semantic descriptor comparison. Its saved expectation and
comparison must include both new bound bit patterns, after existing phase/rate
checks and before evaluation-time checks. Delegating principal and combination
receivers inherit that complete comparison. Preserve signed-zero semantics,
exact-instance reference-input binding and existing failure atomicity. This
change neither resets an invalidated reference owner nor creates observation
credit. No broker identity, journal, reservation, duplicate-operation handling,
clock call, service, Windows command, new dependency or compiler-policy change
belongs in this slice.

Independent required observations for authoring:

- Exact dyadic positive/negative/zero compositions give the manually derived
  sum on a genuine projection while retaining its original history, report,
  source/time metadata and old uncertainty terms.
- Same-domain charts with phase coefficients `2^53`, `1`, `-2^53` along A→B→C→D
  have exact stored-input sum 1, but separately rounded point sums give 0.
  After the second composition, a genuine projection must retain a bound
  enclosing the lost second; a plain zero-displacement descriptor is the useful
  smaller-bound control. Repeat independently for rate at `t-r = 1 second`:
  rate and phase error must retain the lost unit. Already composed left and
  right operands must both preserve inherited error.
- Mismatched intermediate frame/generation, each changed domain field, endpoint
  cycles and finite-operand overflow reject beside valid neighbors, leaving
  both source descriptors/history and prior lifecycle state unchanged. Retain
  the existing subnormal/zero/negative-bound rules and intended error causes.
- Same labels/domain/phase/rate with different coefficient-error bits must
  reject at actual batch, principal and combination consumers. An original
  matching descriptor must still succeed; rejection cannot consume an emitted
  round, principal update or observation credit.
- External/private-construction and result-use controls plus actual Garde
  mechanism, affected executable-line and per-critical-file mutation gates are
  required at the stable qualification checkpoint. Portable authoring alone
  does not close S1.4b. Reuse existing runners and extend only affected accounting
  and admission owners after the source/test census is stable.

Ownership for the next stage: one implementation worker owns `regression/chart.rs`,
`regression/chart/roundoff.rs` and `chart_batch.rs`; an independent test worker
owns the applicable existing public/private chart tests and new composition
fixtures. Root owns this task, integration, configured checks and later
qualification registration. Begin source authoring only after the independent
bounded plan review; no new user approval round is needed for this routine
mathematical extension. Freeze the actual development test/producer census
before running new numerical work and keep initial checks focused. Required
later qualifications, current resource limits and all execution holds remain.

> The proposed change composes two mathematical phase-displacement charts with an identical domain/reference and matching intermediate frame/generation. It adds conservative coefficient-roundoff bounds, carries inherited errors through later compositions and genuine projections, and includes those bounds in descriptor compatibility. ### S1.4b roundoff survivors — one equivalence, two test gaps

mechanism controls remain unexecuted. The separately prepared armed mechanism

Fresh source review `/root/roundoff_survivor_review` inspected the exact three
diffs/logs, checked construction paths and corrected its initial raw-carrier
counterexample before its final verdict. Root independently checked the complete
construction graph and the following exact-domain argument for only
`crates/time-core/src/regression/chart/roundoff.rs:350:33: replace || with && in transform`:

- `transform` receives a checked `FixtureAffineChart`, not an `AffineCandidate`.
  The latter's independent scalar rules return `Result<()>`, without promoting
  an arbitrary carrier to a chart.
- The complete checked chart has two construction paths. `new` installs both
  coefficient errors as positive zero. `compose` obtains both from
  `coefficient_addition_error`; each successful nonnegative outward sum ends
  with `ETA_UPPER = MIN_POSITIVE > 0`. Both published errors are therefore
  positive and finite. Failure publishes no chart. Induction covers arbitrary
  finite successful composition chains.
- Fields are private/immutable; there is no other constructor, setter,
  deserializer, raw promotion or unchecked construction path. The zero tests
  consequently agree on every admitted chart, so `||` and `&&` choose the same
  branch. Pure finite comparisons change no error/effect/lifecycle behavior.
  Signed-zero and overflowing-result cases do not invalidate this argument.
  Any future construction/mutation path requires reconsidering this proof.

The equality changes at columns 26 and 53 are **not equivalent**. For the
existing genuine zero-valued source and a directly constructed zero descriptor,
they unnecessarily apply `sum_two_up(delta_error, 0)`, advancing a positive
bound twice. The original returned center/fitted-phase bound bits are
`0x0034_0000_0000_0005`; either mutant yields `0x0034_0000_0000_0007`.
This violates the explicit direct-descriptor bound-bit compatibility contract.
An independent exact-`Fraction` oracle checked 111 outward operations for
elapsed magnitudes 0, 1.5 and 2.5 seconds and confirmed those constants, with
rate bound `0x0010_0000_0000_0001`; receipt SHA
`820e8b377aee977fa95337d3975827473dafa9102697d0e28bb4a2100e2edeff`.
These are source/mathematical expectations, not new native observations.

Root replaced the existing loose direct-center bound assertion with the exact

ordinary test was added; the production files are unchanged. Test source SHA is

The complete V2 preimage (238 files, 7,876,034 bytes) is retained read-only in

### Next S1.4 receiving contract — reviewed, source implementation ready

Fresh `/root/modeled_phase_plan_review` (`fork_turns: none`) confirmed the
missing quantitative consumer and reviewed the existing readiness, composition
and retained-combination owners. Root adopts its MP-01–MP-04 refinements for
the subsequent pure modeled phase slice. **S1.4b's selected checkpoint is now
closed; this source-only continuation is ready.** This is the existing S1.4 continuation,
not a new product-policy decision or permission for actual clock effects.

- **MP-01, two-stage outcome:** first admit current Ready state, exact predecessor
  chart instance, B frame/generation, complete common domain/reference, supported
  achieved phase scalar, and both B→C/A→C endpoint rules. Rejection at that stage
  preserves state. After admission as a modeled achieved event, commit
  `CoordinateChanged` before fallible replacement composition. Return an admitted
  completion with either its replacement or the exact bounded chart error;
  arithmetic failure cannot masquerade as unchanged-state rejection or leave
  old readiness usable.
- **MP-02, exclusive ownership:** extend `reference_input`; build and consume
  the mandatory Garde-backed checked effect inside one `&mut self` receiving
  call. Do not export an effect token that borrows the owner it must mutate.
  Equal foreign chart instances reject; existing live reference-input borrows
  must exclude this mutable call. Only the achieved quantity enters the numeric
  API; no requested quantity is substituted or required.
- **MP-03, explicit zero/context:** signed zero with a valid distinct B→C
  frame/generation transition remains a modeled coordinate change and invalidates
  old input. `ProvenNoEffect` is the separate identity outcome. Preserve existing
  distinct-tag rules without numeric generation ordering. Domain/reference,
  intermediate/endpoint and unsupported scalar neighbors reject before commit.
- **MP-04, local lifecycle and actual consumer:** repeated achieved feedback on
  the same invalidated owner rejects; Unknown stays dominant. A replacement
  chart restores no old owner and supplies no global/durable replay guarantee.
  Prepare genuine remapped histories, enclosure and batch, then reuse the same
  retained combination owner. Remapping alone must return
  `RetainedWithoutNewEstimate`, without an estimate, round or observation credit.

### Next Core work — modeled active-time rate delivery

Continue the existing development-plan section 7 assignment before dependent
controller, holdover and leap equations. `reference_input` owns receiving and
invalidation; `regression/chart` and its roundoff owner must retain conversion and
product error. Reuse the distinct active-awake and monotonic types. Requested
duration is not delivered time; cancellation uses its known delivered portion,
duplicates add no delivery/observation credit, and unknown outcomes cannot restore
Ready. Actual broker acknowledgements and durable settlement remain later work.

Freeze the interval-to-chart meaning before integration. A proposed restriction
placing the whole chart domain after cessation is not adopted here: reconcile
settled versus ongoing delivery with the existing counterfactual chart and genuine
retained-history contracts first. Never infer active duration by subtracting
monotonic timestamps or silently use a settled constant across an active segment.
The independent dyadic controls are rate 1/1024 for two active seconds = phase
1/512, or one delivered second = 1/1024; monotonic sleep time alone adds nothing.

General admitted binary64 rates also require exact-rational bound-containment
controls and support-bound neighbors. `FixtureAffineChart::new` treats stored
coefficients as exact and sets input roundoff to zero. Therefore a rounded
rate-times-duration result must not flow through the current exact-scalar
`receive_achieved_phase` API. Prepare a private checked point/error representation
through the existing numerical owner and propagate its bound separately from
statistical uncertainty. A current known terminal event must not leave Ready or
allow another consumption merely because later arithmetic/composition failed.
This is the next contract/implementation work, not an already frozen child slice
or a completed ongoing/native slew contract.


### Modeled settled active-time delivery — implementation contract

Continue the existing Core queue after the qualified S1.4c preimage. This is pure
modeled terminal-delivery accounting under REQ-8/10/11/14/15 and the pure parts of
CHECK-7/8/10/14/25, not ongoing physical trajectory or native broker qualification.
The chart is a counterfactual mathematical view: a known settled contribution
`q = applied_rate * (active_end - active_start) / 10^9` becomes a B-to-C constant
translation over the predecessor's SAME complete domain/reference. Compose A-to-C
through the existing chart owner. Retained rows may precede/span the supplied
interval; no domain-after-cessation cutoff is adopted. Monotonic endpoints retain
order/effective-event provenance and never supply elapsed delivery or an implicit
conversion/inequality between clock domains. No requested quantity is an input.

One `FixtureRateClockContext` retains caller-asserted nonzero monotonic origin,
active origin and generation equality tags. `FixtureRateSpan` privately retains
separately ordered typed endpoint pairs and the checked active-awake duration.
`FixtureSettledRateInterval` privately retains that span, context, nonzero local
operation ordinal, supported stored-bit signed rate and checked frames/domain.
Their fallible constructors obligatorily consume shared Garde rules; no skipped
raw fields or public unchecked constructors. Zero duration and signed zero are
supported; they never implicitly become proven no-effect. Numeric support remains
finite normal-or-zero values, with all admitted binary64 rate magnitudes and the
existing exact-integer duration guard of at most 2^53 nanoseconds at integration.

A separate non-Clone `FixtureRateSettlementCursor` retains the expected local
ordinal, context, current mathematical target frame/generation and known endpoint
high-water marks across replacement chart/readiness owners. The ordinal is this
fixture instance's operation identity, not a broker global ID. Adjacency of half-open
spans is valid; strict overlap in either clock rejects. Never wrap a consumed MAX
ordinal: the cursor becomes Exhausted. Unknown makes it Unresolved. Reconstructing
a different fixture cursor supplies no global replay/durable-authority guarantee.

`FixtureReferenceInputState::receive_rate_settlement` receives that cursor, the
exact predecessor and a closed Completed/Cancelled/ProvenNoEffect/Unknown event.
All current events require Ready and exact predecessor identity. Shared admission
checks current cursor/context/ordinal/mapping, complete chart relation, endpoint
chain and known-span overlap before mutation; rejection preserves both owners.
For a known event, consume the ordinal and actual endpoints, update target labels
and invalidate the old owner BEFORE duration conversion, product/error arithmetic
or composition. Later failure belongs inside an opaque admitted completion and
cannot refund/replay or restore Ready. Completed and Cancelled use their actual
modeled delivered portion identically. ProvenNoEffect consumes the local ordinal
without changing coordinates/readiness/high-water marks. Unknown consumes/blocks
the cursor and makes readiness Unknown, with no inferred phase or replacement.
Late/unavailable native-job accounting is not represented by this Ready-only
fixture API; its actual custody, outcome and durable consumption remain broker
obligations. The fixture must not claim those properties.

The numerical owner supplies one crate-private checked settled-rate effect chart.
Compute the exact active integer difference before conversion, then separate
RN(N/10^9) and RN(rate * seconds) through the existing checked arithmetic owner.
Retain an outward absolute error relative to exact stored rate bits times exact
N/10^9; consume it through mandatory AffineCandidate admission, with -0.0 added
rate and zero rate error. Never feed the rounded point into the public exact-input
constructor or expose a raw point/error factory. Exact zero operand cases may have
zero input error with their justified signed-zero point; nonzero subnormal value
results reject under the existing numerical contract. Bounds stay distinct from
statistical deviation, physical uncertainty and native rate quantization.

Independent expected facts: 1/1024 times two active seconds gives 1/512; cancellation
after one active second gives 1/1024; added monotonic elapsed time alone adds no
contribution. For rate bits 3fd5555555555555 and N=3 ns the exact rational has a
factor 5 in its denominator, so a binary64 point cannot equal it and the effect's
pre-composition coefficient bound must be positive. Exercise general rational
containment, negative/zero rates, finite overflow, nonzero subnormal rejection,
2^53 neighbors and large absolute origins. The public receiver must also show
cross-owner duplicate/overlap rejection, unknown/exhaustion monotonicity,
post-admission failure consumption and a genuine retained-history-to-combination
journey with original bits and observation/round credit preserved.

Writable ownership: numerical worker owns regression/chart.rs, chart/roundoff.rs
and existing tests/private/chart_roundoff.rs; the independent fixture worker owns
new tests/rate_delivery.rs. Coordinator owns the new reference_input/rate_delivery.rs
module, shared reference guards, exports and this task. Only the coordinator runs
admitted Rust commands. Focused checks precede a coherent formatted candidate;
then applicable portable, contract, per-file coverage/mutation and validation
mechanism gates remain required. Source-work prepayment and candidate/fixture
counts must be frozen before execution; no old S1.4c score qualifies changed bytes.
All Windows/S134, separate AES-fuzz and storage holds and UI deferrals remain.
